# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-12-03_TotalExtraction_68575538.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
December 9, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook (arrived at 6:08 
p.m.), Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White. Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance 
was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Esquibel moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White. Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 25, 2024. Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar & grill liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Rock Ranch LLC d/b/a Nellie’s, 4020 Airport Parkway, Cheyenne, 
WY.  Public comment was made by: Kathy Brent. Comment was made by the following member 
of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for about 0.13 acres of land located north 
of Cheyenne Street and west of and adjacent to Ridge Road.  Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public 
hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, tracts of 
land situate in portions of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming 
(also known as ‘North Range Business Park’, located northwest of the I-80 and Roundtop Road 
interchange).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4593) 
 
 

 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of LI Light Industrial and BP Business Park for 
tracts of land being annexed to the City situate in portions of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, 6th 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (also known as ‘North Range Business Park’, located northwest 
of the I-80 and Roundtop Road interchange).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. Public comment was made by: Daunte Rushton, Cheyenne LEADS (#4594) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial to AG 
Agricultural for land located south of Campstool Rd and north of Interstate 80.  Mr. Roybal moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. (#4595)  
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, various 
tracts of land completely surrounded by current City limits situate in northeast Cheyenne generally 
located north of Bevans Street, west of College Drive and south of Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#4596) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of MR - Medium-Density Residential, HR - High 
Density Residential, and CB - Community Business for land annexed to the City of Cheyenne 
generally located north of Bevans Street, west of College Drive, and south of Dell Range 
Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4597)   
 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, various 
tracts of land completely surrounded by current City limits situate in northeast Cheyenne generally 
located east of Ridge Road, west of Whitney Road and along Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4598)  
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AR - Agricultural Residential, LR Low-Density 
Residential, MR - Medium-Density Residential, and MUR Mixed-Use Residential for land 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne generally located east of Ridge Road, west of Whitney Road and 
along Dell Range Boulevard.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4599) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public and MUR Mixed-Use Residential to 
MUB Mixed-Use Business for land located northeast of the intersection of Morrie Avenue and 
East Lincolnway.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Public comment 

was made by: Brandon Swain and Dan Dorsch, Executive Director, Habitat for Humanity of 
Laramie County. (#4600) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 8.60.080, Non-Compliant Vehicles 
as a Nuisance, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, declaring oversized 
vehicles as a nuisance, providing for their regulation, clarifying provisions and making conforming 
amendments.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. Esquibel moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Mr. Esquibel moved to approve by substitute dated 
December 4, 2024, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn and 
Mr. Segrave voting “no”. Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by substitute dated December 5, 2024, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion to amend by substitute failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” – 
Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave. Voting “no” – Mayor 
Collins, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White. Mr. White moved to amend page 4, item 
#8 by changing the width requirement from 7 ft. to 8.5 ft., seconded by Dr. Rinne. Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting 
“no”. Dr. Rinne moved to amend page 8, Section 10.52.020(B) by adding the restriction that no 
person shall park where any part of the vehicle extends onto or over any sidewalk or extends onto 
or over the travel lane of a street, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. 
Johnson and Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 10.52.020(B) by 
including to the prior amendment – “for a period of not more than 8 hours and only during daylight 
hours”, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. Main motion as 
amended by substitute, as amended (3 amendments) carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Public comment 
was made by the following: Donald Jones, Lee Filer, Gary Everett, Joseph McCord, Katelyn 
Wilson, Steve Manning, Ed Melko, Alisa Krishtal, Tom Fuller, Daryl Frank, Gregory Barrow, 
James Wenzinger, Terese Johnston, Alan Sheldon, Wayne Gizinski, Georgia Broyles, John Puls, 
Cathy Deister, David Cook, Vicki Nemecek, Nathan Stoner and Jeff Kling.  Comments were made 
by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White. (#4601) 
 
 
 
Mayor Collins reconvened the meeting at 8:23 p.m. after a brief recess.

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner 
Subject: PLTA-24-7 – Amending UDC to modify required façade materials for multifamily buildings. 
Date: 
November 25, 2024 
 
Background:  
The Governing Body heard a similar proposal in late 2023. The Planning Commission in 2023 had 
modified the amendment to propose removing any requirement to vary materials. The Governing Body 
did not approve the amendment as presented by the Planning Commission. However, upon 
reconsideration, the Governing Body approved an amendment to require only variation if the housing 
was income-restricted. The original version as presented to the Planning Commission in 2023 is being 
brought back forward at the specific request of Councilman Dr. Rinne. 
 
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment. This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
11/25/2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
12/3/2024 
• 
Second reading  
 
12/9/2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
12/17/2024 
• 
Third and final reading   
12/23/2024 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their November 18, 2024, meeting and unanimously voted 
to recommend the Governing Body approve the proposed text amendment without modifications. 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed. The Ordinance in this 
packet is not substantively changed from the amendment reviewed by the Planning Commission. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report

(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SUBSECTIONS 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5.c, AND 
6.9.4.e OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE (UDC), CHANGING 
BUILDING FAÇADE DESIGN REQUIREMENTS FOR MULTIFAMILY 
RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENT." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the Unified 
Development Code. 
 
Section 2.  That on November 18, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Subsection 6.6.3.b.3 Materials for Multi-dwelling buildings, of Subsection 
6.6.3.b Building Design, of Subsection 6.6.3 General Residential Design, of Section 6.6 
Residential Design Standards, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this section. 
 
3. Materials for Multi-dwelling buildings. All attached dwelling and multi-dwelling 
building types shall be constructed according to the following material standards. 
Percentage requirements for materials exclude windows or other openings. Carports, 
stand-alone canopies, or other structures which only consist of vertical support posts 
in the area between 2’ and 8’ above grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be 
exempt from the materials requirements of this section. 
(a) Primary Materials. All buildings shall have not less than two primary materials 
covering at least  90% of the building facades.  
No one primary material may cover more than 70% of the facades. No one color may 
cover more than 70% of the facades (colors shall be distinctly different, if there is a 
debate over whether the proposed colors are distinctly different, an applicant may 
appeal the Director’s determination to the Board of Adjustment). 
(b) Accent Materials. Buildings shall have at least one accent or trim material 
covering between 5% and 10% of the building facades. The accent shall be 
a separate material from the primary materials utilized on the building. 
(c) Permitted Materials. Exterior materials shall be permitted in accordance with 
Table 6-18. 
 
Table 6-18: Residential Building Materials 
Primary Materials 
 
Accent / Trim 
Materials 
6:31 pm, Nov 18 2024

Brick, unpainted (or equal or better 
simulated material) 
Stone, unpainted (or equal or better 
simulated material) 
Glass (Opaque) 
Metal Siding & Roofing (non-corrugated) 
Color Concrete 
Stucco (or equal or better simulated material)

better simulated material)

as an accent material

or better simulated

moldings or similar 
architectural 
details (or equal or 
better simulated 
material) 
 
(d) Material Changes. Except for accent materials, changes in the facade material 
shall only occur at horizontal expression lines, with the heavier material below the 
lighter material. Alternatively, changes in the material may occur at differentiated 
structural bays that meet the horizontal massing requirements. 
(e) Equal or Better Simulated Materials. Where “equal or better” simulated materials 
are permitted, they shall only be permitted by the Director as an Administrative 
Adjustment according to procedures and criteria in Article 2. The Director shall use 
the following additional criteria:  
(1) The proposed material has an identical physical appearance as the material it 
intended to simulate, including color and texture, as perceived from any public spaces 
surrounding the property; 
(2) The material presents benefits in addition to cost savings, such as reduced 
maintenance, or enhanced durability and performance; 
(3) The material has a demonstrated track record of successful application and use 
that will result in equally long-lasting and sustainable appeal of the neighborhood as 
that of the permitted materials; and 
(4) Use of equal or better materials may be conditioned upon compliance with all 
application and manufacturer specifications. 
 
Section 4.  That Subsection 6.7.5.c Building Materials, of Subsection 6.7.5 Building 
Design, of Section 6.7 Small Scale Commercial and Mixed-use Design Standards, of Article 6 
Design Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this section. 
 
c. Building Materials. All buildings and accessory buildings over 200 square feet shall be 
constructed according to the following material standards. Percentage requirements for 
materials exclude windows or other openings. Carports, stand-alone canopies, or other 
structures which only consist of vertical support posts in the area between 2’ and 8’ 
above grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be exempt from the materials 
requirements of this section. 

1. Primary Materials. All buildings shall have one primary material covering at least 
30% of the building facades. Buildings which are more than 50% residential dwelling 
units (by square footage) may use any of the Secondary Materials as a primary material 
provided: (a) No less than 2 primary/secondary materials are used;  
(b) No one primary/secondary material covers more than 70% of the facades; and  
(c) No one color covers more than 70% of the facades (colors shall be distinctly different, 
if there is a debate over whether the proposed colors are distinctly different, an applicant 
may appeal the Director’s determination to the Board of Adjustment). 
2. Secondary Materials. Buildings may have a secondary material covering up to 70% of 
the facades.  
3. Accent Materials. Buildings shall have at least one, and may have up to two accent or 
trim materials covering between 5% and 10% of the building facades. The accent shall be 
a separate material from the primary materials utilized on the building. 
4. Permitted Materials. Exterior materials shall be permitted in accordance with  
Table 6-20.

5. Material Changes. Except for accent materials, changes in the façade material shall 
only occur at horizontal expression lines, with the heavier material below the lighter 
material. Alternatively, changes in the material may occur at differentiated structural bays 
that meet the horizontal massing requirements of Sub-section a above. 
6. Equal or Better Simulated Materials. Where “equal or better” simulated materials are 
permitted, they shall only be permitted by the Director as an Administrative Adjustment 
according to procedures and criteria in Article 2. The Director shall use the following 
additional criteria: 
a. The proposed material has an identical physical appearance as the material it 
intended to simulate, including color and texture, as perceived from any 
public spaces surrounding the property; 
b. The material presents benefits in addition to cost savings, such as reduced 
maintenance, or enhanced durability and performance; 

c. The material has a demonstrated track record of successful application and use 
that will result in equally long-lasting and sustainable appeal of the 
neighborhood as that of the permitted materials; and 
d. Use of equal or better materials may be conditioned upon compliance with all 
application and manufacturer specifications. 
 
Section 5.  That Subsection 6.9.4.e Building Materials, of Subsection 6.9.4 Building 
Design, of Section 6.9 General Commercial Design Standards, of Article 6 Design Regulations, of 
the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out 
in this section. 
 
e. Building Materials. All buildings and accessory buildings over 200 square feet shall be 
constructed according to the following material standards. Percentage requirements for 
materials exclude windows or other openings. Carports, stand-alone canopies, or other 
structures which only consist of vertical support posts in the area between 2’ and 8’ 
above grade and are less than 2 stories in height shall be exempt from the materials 
requirements of this section. 
1. Primary Materials. All buildings shall have one primary material covering at least 
30% of the building facades. Buildings which are more than 50% residential dwelling 
units (by square footage) may use any of the Secondary Materials as a primary 
material provided:  
(a) No less than 2 primary/secondary materials are used; 
(b) No one primary/secondary material covers more than 70% of the facades; and 
(c) No one color covers more than 70% of the facades (colors shall be distinctly 
different, if there is a debate over whether the proposed colors are distinctly different, 
an applicant may appeal the Director’s determination to the Board of Adjustment). 
2. Secondary Materials. Buildings may have a secondary material covering up to 70% 
of the façades.  
3. Accent Materials. Buildings shall have at least one, and may have up to two accent or 
trim materials covering between 5% and 10% of the building facades. The accent 
shall be a separate material from the primary materials utilized on the building. 
4. Permitted Materials. Exterior materials shall be permitted in accordance with Table 
6-21.

6. Equal or Better Simulated Materials. Where “equal or better” simulated materials are

(a) The proposed material has an identical physical appearance as the material it

(b) The material presents benefits in addition to cost savings, such as reduced

(c) The material has a demonstrated track record of successful application and use

(d) Use of equal or better materials may be conditioned upon compliance with all

Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
NOVEMBER 18, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5, and 6.9.3.e of the 
Unified Development Code (UDC), changing building façade design requirements for 
multifamily residential development   
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-7 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, AICP Senior Planner, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 6.7.5.c, and 6.9.3.e as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting 
the requests meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the City 
Council is authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.1 to bring forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendment is to change various sections of code regarding façade design 
requirements requiring brick and stone on multifamily buildings. Multifamily buildings in the residential 
and mixed-use zones follow the design requirements found in 6.6. However, multifamily buildings are also 
called ‘Urban Lofts’ in certain scenarios, applying the design requirements of 6.7 in these cases; and 
multifamily buildings are allowed via conditional use in the commercial zones, which would apply the 
design standards of 6.9. Various zone districts also allow mixed-use buildings, which could be used as a 
majority multifamily and in some cases may have multifamily on the ground floor. The code amendment 
proposes removing the requirement for brick and stone for multifamily buildings and bringing clarity to 
the materials requirements. 
 
The current code requires unpainted brick or stone on 30% of the building façades, and does not allow 
these to be replaced with simulated brick or stone materials. The affordable housing task force

PLTA-24-7  
 
 
November 18, 2024 
 
recommended that the materials requirement be modified such that brick or stone was not required on 
multifamily structures.  
 
The requirement for brick and stone is largely aesthetic, as such materials are only a veneer on the 
materials actually used in construction and have little effect on the structure. Brick and stone may be 
more durable than other materials that could be selected for veneer. As a largely aesthetic regulation, 
Staff proposes changing the code to require two distinct materials and two distinct colors, instead of 
requiring brick or stone.  
 
The Planning Commission heard this proposal in late 2023 and modified the amendment to propose 
removing any requirement to vary materials. The Council did not approve the amendment as presented 
by the Planning Commission. However, upon reconsideration, the Council approved an amendment to 
require only variation if the housing was income-restricted. It is being brought back forward at the specific 
request of Councilman Dr. Rinne. 
  
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS:

REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
The Comprehensive plan encourages the development of workforce housing and housing for 
Seniors (Principle 2). PlanCheyenne encourages workforce housing to be made of high-quality 
materials, and with architectural details that are functional and attractive (Policy 2.5.c). Another 
policy states that design standards should be created to address should be high-quality, but no 
specific materials are mentioned. 
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
Regulations; scope and purpose; uniformity within authorized districts; 
to follow plan; objectives. 
  
(a)  The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i)  Regulate and restrict the: 
  
 
(A)  Height, number of stories and size of buildings and other structures; 
(B)  Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
  
 
(C)  Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D)  Density of population; and 
  
 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, industry, 
residence or other purposes. 
  
(ii)  Establish setback building lines. 
  
(b)  The governing body may divide the city or town into districts of such number, shape and area 
as it deems necessary, and within those districts it may regulate and restrict the erection, 
construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair or use of buildings, structures or land. 
  
(c)  Regulations may differ from one (1) district to another but shall be uniform for each class or 
kind of buildings within a district. 
  

PLTA-24-7  
 
 
November 18, 2024 
 
(d)  All regulations shall be made: 
(i)  In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
  
 
(A)  Lessen congestion in the streets; 
  
 
(B)  Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
  
 
(C)  Promote health and general welfare; 
  
 
(D)  Provide adequate light and air; 
  
 
(E)  Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
  
 
(F)  Avoid undue concentration of population; and 
  
 
(G)  Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, water, sewerage, schools, 
parks and other public requirements. 
(ii)  With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character of the district 
and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
  
(iii)  With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the most 
appropriate use of land throughout the city or town; and 
(iv)  With consideration given to the historic integrity of certain neighborhoods or districts 
and a view to preserving, rehabilitating and maintaining historic properties and encouraging 
compatible uses within the neighborhoods or districts, but no regulation made to carry out the 
purposes of this paragraph is valid to the extent it constitutes an unconstitutional taking without 
compensation. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to modify 
sections of code in accordance with the general welfare and character of the community. The 
regulations and the modifications to the regulations are allowed by state statute. 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment would not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. While reducing construction costs, the amendment attempts to still assure the 
aesthetic values of the community are met. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code subsections 6.6.3.b.3, 
6.7.5.c, and 6.9.3.e as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests meets 
the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 2.16.010 OF THE MUNICIPAL 
CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, REQUIRING THE 
CITY TREASURER TO ANNUALLY EVALUATE AND REPORT TO THE 
GOVERNING BODY ANY OUTSTANDING DEBT OWED THE CITY BY 
ANY CITY BOARD, COUNCIL OR COMMISSION MEMBER." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 2.16.010, Duties, of Chapter 2.16, City Treasurer, of Title 2, 
Administration and Personnel, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended to read: 
 
2.16.010 - Duties. 
 
Duties of the city treasurer are to: 
 
A. Receive all money derived for the benefit of the city, from every source, and issue an 
official receipt; 
 
B. Keep permanent, accurate and complete accounts and records of all receipts, orders and 
warrants; 
 
C. If, at any time, any orders and warrants are not paid for lack of funds, the city treasurer 
shall endorse the date of presentation across the back, and the reason for nonpayment; the 
holder of such order or warrant is entitled to receive interest from such date until paid; 
 
D. Endorse upon or attach to every bond or other evidence issued by the city pursuant to law, 
a certificate that it is within the lawful debt limit of the city and is issued pursuant to law, 
and sign such certificates as city treasurer; 
 
E. Prior to the appointment or reappointment of any person to a city board, council or 
commission, review whether that person is overdue on any payments owed to the city, has 
outstanding city debts for services received, or is otherwise liable for any city fines or fees. 
The treasurer shall report the findings of this review to the governing body for 
consideration on whether to approve or deny the appointment or reappointment; and 
 
E. F. 
Perform other duties as may be required by the mayor, members of city council, or 
by law. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-12-03_TotalExtraction_70366337.pdf
 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 8.68, CAMPING ON PUBLIC 
PROPERTY, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, EXPANDING THE GENERAL PROHIBITION 
ON CAMPING TO ALL PUBLIC PROPERTY OF THE CITY, CREATING 
A PERMITTING PROCESS, MAKING CONFORMING AMENDMENTS 
AND REPEALING OBSOLETE PROVISIONS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 8.68.025, Sleeping on Sidewalks, Streets, Alleys or Within 
Doorways Prohibited, and Section 8.68.035, Camping Allowed with Lawful Permit; Permit 
Administration, of Chapter 8.68, Camping on Public Property, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby created to read: 
 
8.68.025 - Sleeping on sidewalks, streets, alleys or within doorways prohibited. 
 
A. No person shall sleep on any public sidewalk, street or alleyway at any time as a matter of 
individual and public safety. 
 
B. No person may sleep in any pedestrian or vehicular entrance to public or private property 
abutting a public sidewalk. 
 
C. Any person who violates this section is guilty of a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of 
fifty dollars ($50.00). Any person found in violation of this section may also be 
immediately removed from the premises. Upon conviction for a violation of this section, if 
a defendant demonstrates that after receiving the citation and before the hearing, they 
meaningfully engaged with public or private resources or service providers to address the 
reasons that led them to be in violation, the court shall consider that information when 
determining the appropriate sentence. 
 
8.68.035 - Camping allowed with lawful permit; permit administration. 
 
A. Notwithstanding the prohibitions imposed under Section 8.68.030 of this chapter, a person 
may camp on public property of the city upon the issuance of a camping permit by the city 
clerk and payment of the associated permit fee as provided by this section. All applications 
for a camping permit shall be submitted to the city clerk on forms provided by the clerk 
and shall be submitted not less than ten (10) business days before the date the camping will 
occur. No permit may be issued under this section except upon the approval of the clerk 
and the director of the community recreation and events department. 
 
B. Before the issuance of any camping permit under this section, the director of the 
community recreation and events department shall: 
 
1:24 pm, Nov 20, 2024

 
Page 2 of 7 
 
1. Determine and keep a record of all parcels of public property of the city that are 
appropriate for camping and for which a camping permit may be issued under this 
section. In identifying any parcel for camping under this paragraph, the director shall 
only select those parcels, and the locations in each parcel, that will not overburden the 
surrounding community in terms of noise, traffic and environmental impact; 
 
2. Prescribe the attendant camping permit fee for camping on a parcel of public property, 
which fee may vary by parcel of public property, but which shall only be in an amount 
sufficient to defer the costs and impacts associated with the camping activity; and 
 
3. Prescribe permit conditions, the intent of which shall be provide for the proper 
maintenance and administration of the parcels upon which camping occurs and to 
protect public health and safety of the camping participants and the community. 
 
C. Any person camping on a parcel of public property without a permit as provided by this 
section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of not more than fifty dollars 
($50.00). Each day a person camps without a permit shall constitute a separate a distinct 
violation of this section. 
 
Section 2.  That Chapter 8.68, Camping on Public Property, of Title 8, Health and Safety, 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended to read: 
 
8.68.010 - Legislative findings and intent.  
 
A. It is found and declared that: 
 
1. The act of camping on public property tends to endanger the health and safety of those 
engaged in such camping as well as the public at large; 
 
2. Campsites positioned on public property of the city of Cheyenne, including on 
undeveloped parcels, public rights-of-way, under bridges and on or near sidewalks, also 
undermine the public's ability to use that property for its intended purpose; 
 
3. Camping on public property also creates unsafe and unsanitary living conditions which 
pose a threat to the peace, health and safety of the people who are camping and the 
community; 
 
4. Crow Creek serves as a vital component of the drainage network of the city of 
Cheyenne, offers valuable habitat for local flora and fauna and the creek's health 
requires special attention; 
 
5. 4. Some individuals have come to increasingly rely on public property adjacent to 
Crow Creek for the purpose of camping and the establishment of campsites creates 
significant hazards for Cheyenne, including damaging water quality through the 
introduction of trash, human waste and other materials; 
 

 
Page 3 of 7 
 
6. 5. Currently portions of Crow Creek are severely Many areas in Cheyenne are 
materially impacted through by camping on public property, demonstrated by the 
presence of trash, discarded items of personal property, discarded shelter elements, and 
human waste; 
 
7. The water quality of Crow Creek is of particular concern with significantly elevated 
levels of E. coli, a sub-group of the fecal coliform group that can cause extraintestinal 
illness and lead to diseases including typhoid fever and cholera; 
 
8. 6. Additionally, continued trash deposits into Crow Creek the waterways of the city 
negatively impact the city of Cheyenne's ability to effectively manage surface water 
runoff because trash can obstruct discharge pipes and otherwise impede the flow of 
surface water through the city's surface water runoff infrastructure; 
 
9. 7. Moreover, camping on public property along Crow Creek not only presents dangers 
to the health and safety of those people camping but poses a threat to persons and 
property, including residents and property owners near those areas; 
 
10. 8. During high and extreme fire conditions, public property along Crow Creek poses 
a can face unique fire danger dangers due to dry brush and other abundant fuel sources, 
and combined with potential unregulated campfires, stoves and other incendiary 
camping supplies, such danger is only increased; 
 
11. It is difficult for emergency personnel to evacuate people camping on public property 
along Crow Creek during a fire event, which places additional strains on the city of 
Cheyenne's emergency response programs and personnel; 
 
12. This chapter's regulations are intended strictly to regulate the use of publicly owned 
property along Crow Creek, and are not intended to regulate activities on private 
property; and 
 
13. 9. The enactment of this chapter is necessary to protect the peace, health, and safety 
of the city and its inhabitants. 
 
8.68.020 - Applicability; definitions. 
 
 
A. This chapter shall not be construed to modify, limit or supersede any requirement imposed 
under the Unified Development Code (UDC). To the extent there is a conflict between this 
chapter and the UDC, the UDC shall govern. 
 
B. As used in this chapter: 
 
1. "Abate" or "abatement" means the removal of all personal property, garbage and other 
foreign materials together with the reclamation and restoration of a campsite to its 
natural or lawful purpose; 
 

 
Page 4 of 7 
 
2. "Campsite" or "camping" means an area where a person temporarily resides or dwells 
through use of a tent, tarpaulin, lean-to, sleeping bag, bedroll, blanket or any other form 
of cover or protection from the elements other than clothing; 
 
3. "City" means as defined by Section 1.04.020 of the city code; 
 
4. "Department" means the compliance department of the city of Cheyenne; 
 
5. "Greater Cheyenne greenway" means the ten-foot wide, reinforced concrete path 
established by the city of Cheyenne that can be utilized by pedestrians and bicyclists 
throughout the city and surrounding areas; 
 
6. 5. "Personal property" means any item that readily appears to belong to a person and 
has apparent value or utility in its present condition. Personal property does not include 
building materials, metal, makeshift shelters, rigid plastic, garbage, contraband, debris 
or waste. 
 
7. 6. "Public property" means any street, alley, sidewalk, bike path, greenway, park, 
parkway or any other structure or area encompassed within the public right-of-way and 
includes any other grounds, buildings or other facilities owned, leased or managed by 
the city, regardless of whether the public property is vacant or occupied and actively 
used for any public purpose; 
 
8. 7. "Reside or dwell" includes, without limitation, conducting such activities as eating, 
sleeping or the storage of personal possessions; and 
 
9. 8. "Unlawful campsite" means any area of public property as specified by Section 
8.68.030(A) of this chapter upon which any person camps engages in the act of 
camping. 
 
8.68.030 - Camping and unlawful campsites on public property prohibited; public property 
occupancy restriction; exception; penalty.  
 
A. It is unlawful for any campsite to be situated on public property adjacent to or contiguous 
to Crow Creek located within the incorporated limits of the city. of Cheyenne between 
Interstate 25 and Morrie Avenue. All Every unlawful campsites campsite shall be subject 
to abatement by the department in accordance with Section 8.68.040 of this chapter. 
 
B. Between the hours of 12:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m., No person shall use or otherwise occupy 
any public property adjacent to or contiguous to Crow Creek located within the 
incorporated limits of the city of Cheyenne between Interstate 25 and Morrie Avenue, 
except as authorized under subsection (C) of this section. for camping purposes unless the 
person holds a valid permit issued in accordance with Section 8.68.035 of this chapter. 
 

 
Page 5 of 7 
 
C. The prohibitions imposed under subsection (B) of this section shall not apply to any person 
actively using the greater Cheyenne greenway located within the subject area as a means 
to commute or otherwise travel for business or for recreational purposes. 
 
D. C. 
Any person who violates subsection (B) of this section is guilty of a misdemeanor 
punishable by imprisonment for not more than six (6) months, a fine of not more than seven 
hundred fifty dollars ($750.00), or both ($50.00). Any person found in violation of 
subsection (B) of this section may also be immediately removed from the premises. Upon 
conviction for a violation of subsection (B) of this section, if a defendant demonstrates that 
after receiving the citation and before the hearing, they meaningfully engaged with public 
or private resources or service providers to address the reasons that led them to be in 
violation, the court shall consider that information when determining the appropriate 
sentence. 
 
8.68.040 - Abatement of unlawful campsites; notice requirement; exception. 
 
A. Any unlawful campsite identified by a law enforcement officer or other Cheyenne city 
employee is subject to abatement by the department. Upon the identification of an unlawful 
campsite the law enforcement officer or Cheyenne city employee shall promptly notify the 
department of the campsite and its location. 
 
B. Upon receiving notice of an unlawful campsite under subsection (A) of this section, the 
department shall assess the condition of the campsite, prioritizing abatement on campsites 
that pose the greatest threat to public health and safety. When the department abates any 
campsite in accordance with this section, the public works departments department shall 
coordinate with the department to provide abatement assistance and support when 
necessary. 
 
C. Except for emergency abatements as authorized under subsection (E) of this section, the 
department may only abate an unlawful campsite after satisfying the following conditions: 
 
1. Providing all persons present at the unlawful campsite verbal or written notice of the 
date and time of the scheduled abatement; 
 
2. Posting notice around the area of the unlawful campsite at intervals sufficient to advise 
any person entering the campsite of the date and time of the scheduled abatement. The 
department shall not be required to repost any previously posted notice that has been 
removed or destroyed prior to the scheduled abatement; and 
 
3. The expiration of a forty-eight- (48) hour notice period following the delivery of the 
notice as required under paragraphs (1) and (2) of this subsection. 
 
D. The notices required under subsection (C) of this section shall include a statement: 
 
1. Advising that both camping on and locating campsites in the area are on public property 
of the city is prohibited under this chapter and that all any person violating this 

 
Page 6 of 7 
 
prohibition is subject to criminal prosecution and that any unlawful campsites are 
campsite is subject to abatement by the city; and 
 
2. That all personal property and any other property, contraband, litter or waste remaining 
at the an unlawful campsite after the expiration of the forty-eight- (48) hour notice 
period shall be considered abandoned and subject to removal and disposal by the city. 
 
E. The required tolling of the forty-eight- (48) hour notice period before an unlawful campsite 
may be abated as required by paragraph (C)(3) of this section shall not apply and the 
campsite may be immediately abated by the department when: 
 
a. 1. There are grounds for law enforcement officials to believe that illegal activities are 
occurring at an established camping site; 
 
b. 2. The chief of the fire department, or his or her designee, determines abatement is 
necessary due to an emergency that presents an imminent threat to real property or to 
the health and safety of any person; or 
 
c. 3. An unlawful campsite is in a watercourse and there is an imminent threat of 
flooding due to a flash flood warning, flood warning or flood watch issued by the 
National Weather Service and confirmed by the city engineer. 
 
F. Any personal property collected by the city because of an emergency abatement as 
authorized under subsection (E) of this section, which property does not constitute evidence 
of a crime, shall be retained by the city for not less than forty-eight (48) hours during which 
time it shall be made reasonably available for and released to the person confirming 
ownership. 
 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND LOCATED NORTH OF CHEYENNE STREET AND 
WEST OF AND ADJACENT TO RIDGE ROAD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
Donna Trumbo, owner of:  
 
Lot 10, Ridge Homes 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; 
 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Jeffrey B. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 5910; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MR 
Medium-density Residential by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
6:23 pm, Dec 02 2024

Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY
DECEMBER 9, 2024
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-198: 3410 Ridge Road - Annexation
REQUEST:
An annexation of 0.13 acres of land
LOCATION:
North of Cheyenne Street and west of and adjacent to Ridge Road.
APPLICANT:
Shane Hanson, Stiel Surveying
AGENT:
Shane Hanson, Stiel Surveying
OWNERS:
Donna Trumbo
PURPOSE:
Connect to City utilities.
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-198 
 
 
December 9, 2024 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing 0.13 acres of land to allow a connection to City water and sewer. The 
land is currently used as a single-family home. The annexed area will be assigned a City zone based on the 
underlying County zone. No other applications have been submitted for this property at this time.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by an assigned 
City zone of MR Medium-density Residential zone.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. By being within City Limits the property will be able to connect to City 
services.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property is developed with a single-family home on a parcel smaller than those currently 
allowed by the County, this is urban development that already is a natural, geographic, 
economic, and social part of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the south, east, and

PUDC-24-198 
 
 
December 9, 2024 
west. The contiguity to the City and the proximity to surrounding urban-style development 
makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The annexation of this property will 
benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is 
Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Most utilities are within the area.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 75% contiguous.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on November 1, 2024. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for December 9, 2024. Twenty (20) business days 
(on the City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is November 6, 2024. The earliest 
confirmation of delivery was dated November 4, 2024. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-198 
 
 
December 9, 2024 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with urban residential uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access 
to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban 
Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
No major comments were received.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE 
ESTABLISHING 
THE 
ZONING 
CLASSIFICATION OF MR MEDIUM-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL FOR 
LAND ANNEXED TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE LOCATED NORTH OF 
CHEYENNE STREET AND WEST OF AND ADJACENT TO RIDGE 
ROAD.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  Donna Trumbo, owner of: 
 
Lot 10, Ridge Homes 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property 
described herein and to make it a part thereof. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on December 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as MR Medium-
density Residential. 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 5 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
6:20 pm, Dec 02 2024

 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-198 
 
 
December 2, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for Lot 10, Ridge Homes to MR 
Medium-density Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets 
the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant has petitioned for Lot 10, Ridge Homes to be annexed into city limits. Annexation of a parcel 
requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone will be assigned based on the underlying county 
zone and per the policy document adopted by the city. Should the applicant wish to have a different zone at 
the time of annexation, they will be required to submit a subsequent zoning map amendment, proposing a 
different zone. The applicant is not requesting a different City zone at this time. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zone is MR Medium-density Residential. The most analogous city zone is 
MR Medium-density Residential according to the policy document. The Assigned Zoning policy 
document is attached to the staff report as attachment number two.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  

PUDC-24-198 
 
 
December 2, 2024 
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for Lot 10, Ridge Homes to MR Medium-density 
Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria 
for approval.

2. Assigned Zoning Policy Document

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE 
ESTABLISHING 
THE 
ZONING 
CLASSIFICATION OF AG AGRICULTURAL
FOR LAND BEING 
ANNEXED TO THE CITY AT LOT 1, BLOCK 1, NORTH RANGE 
SUBSTATION, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (LOCATED SOUTH OF 
HAPPY JACK ROAD, WEST OF BRUNS WAY)." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the City of Cheyenne is initiating the annexation of property, including 
property legally described as: 
Lot 1, Block 1, North Range Substation, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city. 
Section 3. That on December 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning assignment.
Section 4. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
Section 5. That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as AG
Agricultural. 
Section 6. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
Section 7. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
Section 8. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

December 2, 2024
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land being annexed (as 
shown in the attached annexation map) located northwest of the intersection of Roundtop Road and  
I-80 to AG Agricultural for land currently zoned County A-2 Agricultural, noting that the project meets 
the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The City of Cheyenne is initiating the annexation of the North Range Business Park. This business park has 
been using Cheyenne water and sewer service as an outside user. On typical outside user agreements 
(including the ones entered into here), annexation when eligible is a condition. Recent annexation south of 
this property has made the property eligible for annexation. Due to an oversight with the required notice, the 
County A-2 zoned parcel was overlooked. This project is only for the parcel zoned A-2 Agricultural, as all other 
parcels already have been through the assigned zoning process. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county 
zones for the area being 
annexed are County LI 
Light Industrial, County 
PUD Planned Unit 
Development, and 
County A-2 Agricultural. 
The LI and PUD zoned 
areas were assigned City 
zoning with a separate 
application. The most 
analogous city zone for 
the County A-2 zone is 
AG Agricultural. The 
Assigned Zoning policy 
document is attached to 
the staff report as 
attachment number two.  
 
This project complies with review criterion 1. 
Current Zoning 

PUDC-24-89
December 2, 2024
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for land being annexed northwest of the intersection 
of Roundtop Road and I-80 to AG Agricultural, noting that the project meets the review criteria for 
approval.

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY 
OF

CLASSIFICATION OF AG AGRICULTURAL FOR LAND LOCATED 
NORTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF WEST ALLISON ROAD AND 
WALTERSCHEID BOULEVARD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That the property does not currently have a City zone. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on December 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of 
this ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as:

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
DECEMBER 2, 2024
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-24-216: Allison Pointe – Zoning Map Amendment (zone change)
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 14.47 acres of land from County A-1
to AG Agricultural.
LOCATION:
Northwest of the intersection of W Allison Road and Waltersheid Blvd. 
APPLICANT:
John Sayers
AGENT:
  John Sayers
OWNER:
Tru Grit Land Development LLC
ZONING:
County A-1
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE:
To give the property a City zone and allow a temporary Work Camp via 
Conditional Use.
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-24-216 
 
 
December 2, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of 14.47 acres of land northwest of the intersection of W Allison Road 
and Waltersheid Boulevard as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets 
the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
x 
UDC Article 5 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County A-1 to AG Agricultural for roughly 
14.47 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. This parcel was annexed to the City in 1890. Since then it has 
had a County zone and was never given a City zone when the City and County separated. The applicant is 
proposing the AG zone to allow a temporary Work Camp through the Conditional Use process. If a property 
with County A-1 zoning was annexed to the City, the assigned zoning upon annexation would be AG 
Agricultural. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure.  
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-24-216 
 
 
December 2, 2024 
While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the land uses identified by the Future Land 
Use Map, the AG zone is the rough equivalent of the current County zone and would not have 
an adverse effect on the potential for the property to develop in accordance with the plans and 
policies of the comprehensive plan in the future. The AG zone is also intended to be a ‘holding 
zone’. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The future land uses anticipated by the Comprehensive Plan for this area anticipate mixed-use 
commercial types of development. While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the 
Future Land Use Map, the AG zone is intended to be able to be used as a ‘holding zone’ until 
future development occurs, and the proposed AG zone is consistent with the existing County 
zoning.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current zone is County A-1 which, outside of City limits, allows for limited development 
options and is intended for lower intensity uses. Work Camps are a conditional use in the 
County A-1 zone. The AG zone is the most analogues City zone to the current County zone. If this 
parcel was to be annexed under current UDC regulations it would have been assigned the AG 
zone.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in a pre-existing neighborhood. No changes to the street network 
are proposed. Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the area. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The AG zone is intended to develop with primarily agricultural uses or to leave land in a natural 
state. This zone prohibits most of the typical urban-style uses.  The AG zone is applicable to 
areas where significant natural lands or open spaces are to be protected, or areas designated 
Agriculture/Rural or Rural Residential by the Future Land Use Map. The AG zone is also 
applicable as an interim or holding zone for areas that may be further developed in the long 

UDC-24-216 
 
 
December 2, 2024 
term, but where minimal development activity in the interim is acceptable and will not 
prematurely establish development patterns. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposal which is the impetus behind this request is a Work Camp. A 
Work Camp is a conditional use in the County A-1 zone and the City AG zone. The proposed AG 
zoning is the zone most similar to the existing zoning on the property. More intense 
development on the property zoned AG would require a future rezoning.   
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Planning and Development Department Staff would like to note that if this property was to be 
annexed and assigned a City zone under today’s code, it would have been assigned the AG zone.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
AG zone at this location – as it would be the ‘assigned zoning’ equivalent to the existing County zone.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application for the AG zone meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  
The AG zone is the most analogous City zone to the current County zone and would have been assigned 
to the property if it were to be annexed under current code requirements. 
 
 

UDC-24-216 
 
 
December 2, 2024 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County A-1 to AG. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Differences in uses per zone

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CH EYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT (PUD) (ORDINANCE #4274) 
TO NR-3 NEIGHBORHOOD RESIDENTIAL – HIGH DENSITY FOR 
LAND LOCATED NORTH OF WEST COLLEGE DRIVE, SOUTH OF 
WEST 
ALLISON 
ROAD, 
AND 
WEST 
OF 
WALTERSCHEID 
BOULEVARD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on December 2, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of 
this ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 1, Block 10, Harmony Valley, 1st Filing and Lot 1, Block 10, Interior Heights, 
4th Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming is hereby changed to NR-3 Neighborhood 
Residential – High Density. 
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 44.16 acres of land from PUD to NR-3 
Neighborhood Residential-High Density.

John Sayers

  John Sayers
